. We also add an edge between
v_{i_{2}}
and the remaining parent of
v_{i_{1}}
. To stay consistent with the definition of our tree topology, we also rename nodes.
More formally:

V^{\prime}=V\setminus\{u\in V\mid v_{i_{1}}\text{ is a prefix of }u\}\cup\{v_{i_{1}}\cdot w\mid w\in{\rm Nature}^{\ast}\text{ and }v_{i_{2}} \cdot w\in V\}
, and
E^{\prime}=\{(w,w\cdot x)\mid x \in{\rm Nature},w\in V^{\prime},w\cdot x\in V^{\prime}\}
.
Let
w\in V^{\prime}
, we let

\displaystyle\textsf{tr}:V^{\prime}

\displaystyle\rightarrow V

\displaystyle\textsf{tr}:w

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{cases}w&\textrm{ if $v_{i_{1}}$ is not a prefix of $w$ in $V^{\prime}$}\\
v_{i_{2}}\cdot w^{\prime}&\textrm{ if $w=v_{i_{1}}\cdot w^{\prime}$ in $V^{\prime}$ for some $w^{\prime}\in{\rm Nature}^{\ast}$}.\end{cases}

Note that for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, such that
(u_{1},u_{2})\neq(v_{i_{1}-1},v_{i_{1}})
, it holds that:
(u_{1},u_{2})\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
if and only if
(\textsf{tr}(u_{1}),\textsf{tr}(u_{2}))\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
.

We prove the existence of an execution covering
q_{f}
with
\Gamma^{\prime}
in three steps.

First step.

We start by building
C^{\prime}_{0}=(\Gamma^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{0})\in\mathcal{I}
,
C^{\prime}_{1},\dots,C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})}\in\mathcal{C}
such that for all
0\leq i\leq b(v_{i_{2}})
, and
C^{\prime}_{i}=(\Gamma^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{i})
, for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
L^{\prime}_{i}(u)=L_{i}(\textsf{tr}(u))
.
We now prove by induction that

C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow^{\ast}\cdots\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})}
.
Let
k\geq 0
, and assume that we have proved that
C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{k}
.

Assume that
C_{k}\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{k+1}
.

•

if
t=(q,\tau,q^{\prime})
and there exists
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(v)=u
, then by construction,
L^{\prime}_{k}(v)=L_{k}(v)=q
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v)=L_{k+1}(u)=q^{\prime}
. For all other nodes
v^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(v^{\prime}))=L_{k}(\textsf{tr}(v^{\prime}))=L^{\prime}_{k}(v^{\prime})
as
tr
is injective. Hence,
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v,t}C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

•

if
t=(q,\tau,q^{\prime})
and there is no
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(v)=u
, then for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v)=L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(v))=L_{k}(\textsf{tr}(v^{\prime}))=L^{\prime}_{k}(v)
and so
C^{\prime}_{k+1}=C^{\prime}_{k}
.

•

if
t=(q,!!m,q^{\prime})
and there
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(v)=u
, then by construction,
L^{\prime}_{k}(v)=L_{k}(v)=q
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v)=L_{k+1}(u)=q^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, for all nodes
v^{\prime}
such that
(v^{\prime},v)\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, then either
(\textsf{tr}(v^{\prime}),\textsf{tr}(v))\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
or
\{v,v^{\prime}\}=\{v_{i_{1}-1},v_{i_{1}}\}
.
In the first case, as
C_{k}\xrightarrow{\textsf{tr}(v),t}C_{k+1}
, either
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v^{\prime}),?m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v^{\prime}))\in\Delta
or
L^{\prime}_{k}(v^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v^{\prime})
and there is no
p\in Q
such that
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v^{\prime}),?m,p)\in\Delta
. In the latter case, recall that
k<b(v_{i_{2}})
and, from
Lemma

45
,
b(v_{i_{2}})<\cdots<b(v_{i_{1}})<b(v_{i_{1}-1})
. Hence,
u\nin\{v_{i_{2}},v_{i_{1}-1}\}
. Hence,
v\nin\{v_{i_{1}-1},v_{i_{1}}\}
, and we reach a contradiction.

All other nodes
u^{\prime}
not neighbors of
v
are such that
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime}))
and
(\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime}),\textsf{tr}(v))\nin\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, as
v\nin v_{i_{1}}
. Hence,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime}))=L_{k}(\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime}))=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
.

Hence
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v,t}C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

•

if
t=(q,!!m,q^{\prime})
and there is no
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(v)=u
, hence
u=v_{i_{1}}\cdot x
for some
x\in{\rm Nature}^{\ast}
and
v_{i_{2}}
is not a prefix of
u
.
We prove that for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
, it holds that
L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(v))=L_{k}(\textsf{tr}(v))
. Assume this is not the case, then
(\textsf{tr}(v),u)\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
.
If
\textsf{tr}(v)
is a parent of
u
and so
\textsf{tr}(v)=v_{i_{1}-1}
and
u=v_{i_{1}}
. With
Lemma

45
it implies that
k<b(v_{i_{2}})<b(u)
, which is absurd as
C_{k}\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{k+1}
.
If
u
is a parent of
\textsf{tr}(v)
, then
\textsf{tr}(v)=v_{i_{2}}
and
u=v_{i_{2}-1}
. For the same reason, we get
k<b(v_{i_{2}})<b(u)
, which is absurd as
C_{k}\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{k+1}
. Hence
(\textsf{tr}(v),u)\nin\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
and
L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(v))=L_{k}(\textsf{tr}(v))
. Hence,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v)=L_{k+1}(\textsf{tr}(v))=L_{k}(\textsf{tr}(v))=L^{\prime}_{k}(v)
for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
and so
C^{\prime}_{k}=C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

Hence
C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})}
where for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
L^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})}(u)=L_{b(v_{i_{2}})}(\textsf{tr}(u))
.

Second step.

Next, we build
C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})+1},C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})+2},\dots,C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})}\rightarrow^{\ast}\cdots\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})}
. Moreover, we ensure that for all
b(v_{i_{2}})\leq k\leq b(v_{i_{1}})
, if we let
C^{\prime}_{k}=(\Gamma^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{k})
,

\displaystyle\textrm{for all }u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})\textrm{ such that }u=\textsf{tr}(u),L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)

\displaystyle\textrm{ and }L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=q_{i_{2}}.

Observe that, from the first step,
C^{\prime}_{b(u_{i_{2}})}
is such that for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
L^{\prime}_{b(u_{i_{2}})}(u)=L_{b(u_{i_{2}})}(\textsf{tr}(u))
, and then

L^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{2}})}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{b(v_{i_{2}})}(\textsf{tr}(v_{i_{1}}))=L_{b(v_{i_{2}})}(v_{i_{2}})=q_{i_{2}}
. So the induction hypothesis holds for
k=b(v_{i_{2}})
.

Now, let
b(u_{i_{2}})\leq k<b(u_{i_{1}})
and assume that we have built
C^{\prime}_{b(u_{i_{2}})}
, …,
C^{\prime}_{k}
. Let
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)

and
t=(q,\alpha,q^{\prime})
such that
C_{k}\xrightarrow{v,t}C_{k+1}
. If there is no
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(u)=v
, or if the only
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})

such that
\textsf{tr}(u)=v
is such that
u\neq v
, then we let
C^{\prime}_{k+1}=C^{\prime}_{k}
.

•

If
\alpha=\tau
, we know that for all
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)\setminus\{v\}
,

L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})
, hence, by induction hypothesis, if
u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
, we have that
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
, and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=q_{i_{2}}
.

•

If
\alpha=!!m
for some
m\in\Sigma
, then the neighbors of
v
may have changed state. Since
k<b(v_{i_{1}})
, we know that
u\neq v_{i_{1}}
. Hence,
u=v_{i_{1}}\cdot w
for some
w\in{\rm Nature}^{+}
and then, for all
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, if

u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
, then
u^{\prime}\notin\textsf{N}_{\Gamma}(v)
. Thus, for all
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
,
L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})
and
then
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
. Moreover,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=q_{i_{2}}
.

Assume now that
v=\textsf{tr}(v)
. By definition,
v_{i_{1}}
is not a prefix of
v
in
V^{\prime}
.

•

If
\alpha=\tau
, we let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v)=q^{\prime}
and, for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})\setminus\{v\}
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)=L_{k}(u)
. By induction hypothesis,
L^{\prime}_{k}(v)=L_{k}(v)
, hence
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v),\tau,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v))\in\Delta
and
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v,t}C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.
Moreover, for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
u=\textsf{tr}(u)
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
. Also,
v\neq v_{i_{1}}
hence
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=q_{i_{2}}
.

•

If
\alpha=!!m
, let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v)=q^{\prime}
and, for all
u\in\textsf{N}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)
, if
\textsf{tr}(u)=u
, let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
. For all other
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L^{\prime}_{k}(u)
. First, we show that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v,t}C^{\prime}_{k+1}
. By induction hypothesis,
L^{\prime}_{k}(v)=L_{k}(v)
, hence
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v),!!m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v))\in\Delta
. Let
u\in\textsf{N}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)
and assume that
\textsf{tr}(u)\neq u
. It means that
u=v_{i_{1}}\cdot w
for some
w\in{\rm Nature}^{*}
. Since

v=\textsf{tr}(v)
, we also know that
v_{i_{1}}
is not a prefix of
v
.
If
v=u\cdot x
then
v=v_{i_{1}}\cdot w\cdot x
, which is impossible since
u_{i_{1}}
is not a prefix of
v
. If
u=v\cdot x
, then either
v_{i_{1}}
is also
a prefix of
v
which is impossible, or
u=v_{i_{1}}
and then
v_{i_{1}}=v\cdot x
. This means that
v=v_{i_{1}-1}
, the father of
v_{i_{1}}
. By
Lemma

45
, it would mean that
b(v)=b(v_{i_{1}})<b(v)
but
k<b(v_{i_{1}})
and
v
broadcasts a message from
C_{k}
.
This leads to a contradiction. Hence, for all
u\in\textsf{N}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)
,
\textsf{tr}(u)=u
, and then, for all
u\in\textsf{N}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
. By induction hypothesis,
L_{k}(u)=L^{\prime}_{k}(u)
, then either
L_{k}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
and there is no
(L^{\prime}_{k}(u),?m,p)\in\Delta
, or
(L^{\prime}_{k}(u),?m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u))
. Hence

C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v,t}C^{\prime}_{k+1}
. Moreover, by induction hypothesis, for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(u)=u
,
L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)
. Then, if
u\in\textsf{N}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
, and otherwise,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)
. Let again
u
such that
u=\textsf{tr}(u)
and
(u,v)\notin\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, then
(\textsf{tr}(u),\textsf{tr}(v))\notin\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
, hence
(u,v)\notin\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
. Then
u\notin\textsf{N}_{\Gamma}(v)
and
L_{k+1}(u)=L_{k}(u)
. Hence, for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})

such that
u=\textsf{tr}(u)
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
. Since
v_{i_{1}}\neq\textsf{tr}(v_{i_{1}})
, and since
v\neq v_{i_{1}}
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=q_{i_{2}}
by induction hypothesis.

In this part of the execution, we have forgotten the transitions issued by nodes
u=v_{i_{1}}\cdot w
for
w\in{\rm Nature}^{+}
in
\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
. The configuration
we reach is such that
L^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})}(v_{i_{1}})=q_{i_{2}}
and correspond to the states reached in
C_{b(v_{i_{1}})}
in the rest of the tree. This will allow us
to reach
q_{f}
. The subtree rooted in
v_{i_{1}}
might visit different states than in the original execution, but this will not influence the states visited
by the root, thanks to the fact that the protocol is 2-phase bounded.

Third step.

Finally, we build
C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})},C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})+1},\dots,C^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})+1}\rightarrow^{\ast}\cdots\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{n}
. Moreover, we ensure that for all
b(v_{i_{1}})\leq k\leq n
, if we let
C^{\prime}_{k}=(\Gamma^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{k})
,

\displaystyle\textrm{for all }u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})\textrm{ such that }u=\textsf{tr}(u),L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)

\displaystyle\textrm{ and }L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\textrm{ or }L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}.

Processes
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
u=\textsf{tr}(u)
, perform the same sequence of transitions than between
C_{b(v_{i_{1}})}
and
C_{n}
and process
v_{i_{1}}
broadcasts the sequence of messages broadcast by the same node in
\Gamma
.
Formally we build the sequence by induction on
b(v_{i_{1}})\leq k\leq n
.
C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})}
is already defined and satisfies the induction property.
Assume we have built
C^{\prime}_{b(v_{i_{1}})},\dots,C^{\prime}_{k}
for some
b(v_{i_{2}})\leq k<n
such that:
for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
with
u=\textsf{tr}(u)
, it holds that
L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)
,
and, furthermore either
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
or
L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
.

Denote
C_{k}\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{k+1}
with
t=(q,\alpha,q^{\prime})
and
\alpha\in\{\tau\}\cup!!\Sigma
.

•

First, if
\alpha=\tau
and
u=\textsf{tr}(u)
or
u=v_{i_{1}}
, then define
L^{\prime}_{k+1}
as follows: if
L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)
, define
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=q^{\prime}
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
for all other nodes, otherwise
L^{\prime}_{k+1}=L^{\prime}_{k}
.

–

If
L^{\prime}_{k}(u)=L_{k}(u)
,
L_{k}(u)=q
and so we immediately get that
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{k+1}
. Furthermore, for all
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})\setminus\{u\}
, it holds that
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
. If
u^{\prime}\neq v_{i_{1}}
and
u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
, by induction hypothesis,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
.
Furthermore, either
u=v_{i_{1}}
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
, or
u\neq v_{i_{1}}
and as
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
, it holds that
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
or
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
.

–

If
L^{\prime}_{k}(u)\neq L_{k}(u)
, by induction hypothesis
u=v_{i_{1}}

and
L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
. As
P
is 2-phase-bounded, for all
p
such that
(L_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),\tau,p)\in\Delta
, it holds that
p\in Q_{2}^{r}
. Hence,
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
and the induction property holds.

Note that if
u\neq\textsf{tr}(u)
and
u\neq v_{i_{1}}
, for all
u^{\prime}
such that
u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
or
u^{\prime}=v_{i_{1}}
, it holds that
L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})
, hence by taking
C^{\prime}_{k+1}=C^{\prime}_{k}
, the induction property holds.

•

In the following, we assume that
\alpha=!!m
for some
m\in\Sigma
.
We start by defining
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
, then we prove that
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{k+1}
and finally we prove that
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
respects the induction property. We build
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
depending on
u
.

1.

if
\textsf{tr}(u)=u
and
u\neq v_{i_{1}-1}
, then let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
for all
u^{\prime}
such that
\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
, and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
for all other nodes.

2.

if
u=v_{i_{1}-1}
, then let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
for all
u^{\prime}
such that
\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
, and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
for all other nodes
u^{\prime}\neq v_{i_{1}}
. It is left to define
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
. If
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
, then define
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
, otherwise if there exists
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),?m,p)\in\Delta
, define
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=p
, and else define
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
.

3.

if
u=v_{i_{1}}
, then let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=q^{\prime}
, and let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
for all
u^{\prime}
such that
\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
. Furthermore, for all other nodes
u^{\prime}
, if
(v_{i_{1}},u^{\prime})\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, then if there exists
(L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime}),?m,p)\in\Delta
, let
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=p
, otherwise
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
. If
(v_{i_{1}},u^{\prime})\nin\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, then
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
.

4.

otherwise,
C^{\prime}_{k+1} =C^{\prime}_{k}
.

We prove now that in all cases 1, 2 and 3, it holds that
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

In case 1, observe that as
u\neq v_{i_{1}-1}
and
\textsf{tr}(u)=u
, then all neighbors
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{N}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(u)
are such that
\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
. Indeed, if
v_{i_{1}}
is not a prefix of
u
, it is not a prefix of its parent (if any), and for
x\in{\rm Nature}
,
v_{i_{1}}
is a prefix of
u\cdot x
if and only if
u\cdot x=v_{i_{1}}
, i.e.
u=v_{i_{1}-1}
. Hence, for all
(u^{\prime},u)\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, it holds that
(\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime}),\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime}))=(u,u^{\prime})\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
, and by induction hypothesis
L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})
. Therefore, as
C_{k}\rightarrow C_{k+1}
,
(L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime}),?m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime}))\in\Delta
or there is no transition labeled by
?m
from
L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
.
Furthermore,
(L^{\prime}_{k}(u),!!m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u))\in\Delta
. Let
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
which is not a neighbor of
u
, and such that
\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
. Then,
u^{\prime}\notin\textsf{N}_{\Gamma}(u)
, and by induction hypothesis
L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})
. As
C_{k}\rightarrow C_{k+1}
,
L_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
. And so by definition of
L^{\prime}_{k+1}
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
. Let
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
which is not a neighbor of
u
and such that
\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})\neq u^{\prime}
. Then, by definition of
L^{\prime}_{k+1}
,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
.
Hence
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

In case 2, the only difference is that
v_{i_{1}}
is now a neighbor of the broadcasting node
u=v_{i_{1}-1}
. If
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
, then we defined
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
. As
(v_{i_{1}-1},v_{i_{1}})\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
and
C_{k}\rightarrow C_{k+1}
, then
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),?m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}}))\in\Delta
or there is no transition labeled by
?m
from
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
. Hence,
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.
Otherwise,
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\neq L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
, and by definition of
L^{\prime}_{k+1}
, either
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),?m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}}))\in\Delta
, or
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
and there is no transitions
(L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),?m,p)\in\Delta
for some
p\in Q
. Hence,
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

In case 3, note that by definition of a 2-phase-bounded protocol,
L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\nin Q_{2}^{r}
as
(L_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),!!m,q^{\prime})\in\Delta
. Hence, by induction hypothesis,
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
and so
v_{i_{1}}
can perform the broadcast of
m
from
C^{\prime}_{k}
.
Furthermore, by induction hypothesis, its parent
v_{i_{1}-1}
is such that
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}-1})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}-1})
and, by definition,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}-1})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}-1})
. As
(v_{i_{1}-1},v_{i_{1}})\in\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
, and
C_{k}\rightarrow C_{k+1}
, either
(L_{k}(v_{i_{1}-1}),?m,L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}-1}))\in\Delta
or there is no transitions
(L_{k}(v_{i_{1}-1}),?m,p)\in\Delta
for some
p\in Q
.
For other neighbors
u^{\prime}
of
v_{i_{1}}
,
u^{\prime}\neq\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
and by construction of
L^{\prime}_{k+1}
,
(L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime}),?m,L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime}))\in\Delta
, or there is no reception transitions labeled by
?m
from
L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
. Other nodes
u^{\prime}
which are not neighbors of
v_{i_{1}}
are either such that
u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
, and it holds that
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
, as
(u^{\prime},v_{i_{1}})\nin\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
. Or
u^{\prime}\neq\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
and by construction,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})
.
Hence it holds that
C^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

We prove now that in all cases 1, 2, 3 and 4,
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
satisfies the induction property, i.e. for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
\textsf{tr}(u)=u
, it holds that
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u)=L_{k+1}(u)
, and
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
or
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
. In case 1, the first part is trivial by construction. As
(u,v_{i_{1}})\nin\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
,
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
, hence either
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
by induction hypothesis, or
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
.

In case 2, either
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})=L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})
or
L^{\prime}_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\neq L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})
. In the latter case, by induction hypothesis,
L_{k}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
, and so, as
P
is 2-phase-bounded,
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
.
Case 3 is direct from the definition of
L^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

In case 4, for all
u^{\prime}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
such that
u^{\prime}=\textsf{tr}(u^{\prime})
,
L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})
as
(u^{\prime},u)\nin\textsf{E}(\Gamma)
. Hence,
L^{\prime}_{k+1}(u^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k}(u^{\prime})=L_{k+1}(u^{\prime})
. However, if
u=v_{i_{1}+1}
,
v_{i_{1}}
might change its state between
C_{k}
and
C_{k+1}
upon reception of
m
. If it does so,
(L_{k}(v_{i_{1}}),?m,L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}}))\in\Delta
. As
k>b(v_{i_{1}})
and
P
is 2-phase-bounded,
v_{i_{1}}
already went through its broadcasting phase,
and
L_{k+1}(v_{i_{1}})\in Q_{2}^{r}
, and so the induction property holds.

Conclusion.

Hence we found an execution covering
q_{f}
with a tree topology
\Gamma^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime})
and
|V^{\prime}|<|V|
. It contradicts the fact that
|V|=f(P,q_{f})
.

\square

E.1.2
Proof of
Theorem

19

Proof of
Theorem

19
.

We prove the decidability by reducing both
Cover
and
{\textsc{Cover}[\textsf{Trees}]}
to
\textsc{Cover}[K-\textsf{BP}]
. Let
P
a protocol and
q_{f}
a state of
P
.
Assume that there is a topology
\Gamma
with which

q_{f}
is coverable. Then, by
Theorem

4
, there exist a tree topology
\Gamma^{\prime}
with wich
q_{f}
is coverable. Assume that

|V|=f(P,q_{f})
.
Then, each simple path in
\Gamma^{\prime}
has a length bounded by
2(|Q|+1)
.
Indeed, let
u_{1},u_{2}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, as
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a tree topology, the unique simple path between
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
either contains
\epsilon
and
d(u_{1},u_{2})=|u_{1}|+|u_{2}|
, or there exists
u\in{\rm Nature}^{+}
such that
u_{1}=u\cdot u_{1}^{\prime}
and
u_{2}=u\cdot u^{\prime}_{2}
, and
u_{1}^{\prime}
and
u_{2}^{\prime}
have no common prefix. In the latter case, the length of the simple path between
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
is
|u_{1}^{\prime}|+|u_{2}^{\prime}|<|u_{1}|+|u_{2}|
. In both cases,
d(u_{1},u_{2})\leq|u_{1}|+|u_{2}|
, and hence from
Lemma

18
,
d(u_{1},u_{2})\leq 2(|Q|+1)
. Then
q_{f}
is coverable in a
2(|Q|+1)
-bounded path topology.

Conversely, assume that there exists a
2(|Q|+1)
-bounded path topology
\Gamma
with which
q_{f}
is coverable. Then, immediately, there is a topology with which

q_{f}
is coverable. By
Theorem

4
, there also exists a tree topology with which
q_{f}
is coverable.
Theorem

17

allows to conclude to decidability of both
Cover
and
{\textsc{Cover}[\textsf{Trees}]}
.

\square

E.2
Proofs of
Section

6.2

E.2.1
Proof of
Lemma

20

Let
q_{f}\in Q
be a coverable state.
We fix
\Gamma
with
\textsf{V}(\Gamma)=\{v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
the line topology such that there exist
\rho=C_{0}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow C_{n}
with
C_{n}=(\Gamma,L_{n})
and
L_{n}(v_{N})=q_{f}
for some
v_{N}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
. We suppose wlog that
N\geq 3
and
N\leq\ell-2
, otherwise, we can just add artificial nodes not issuing any transition in the execution.

We start by proving the following lemma.

Lemma 46
.

There exists
\rho^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{n}
such that
C^{\prime}_{n}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime}_{n})
with
L^{\prime}_{n}(v_{N})=q_{f}
, and

•

for all
1\leq i\leq N-2
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime})
;

•

for all
N+2\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime})
.

Proof of
Lemma

46
.

To prove
Lemma

46
, we give two symmetric lemmas.

Lemma 47
.

Let
\rho=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
with
C=(\Gamma,L)
and
1\leq K<\ell
.
There exists
\rho^{\prime}=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}
such that
C^{\prime}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime})
, and

•

for all
v\in\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
,
L^{\prime}(v)=L(v)
;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq K-1
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime})
;

•

for all
K\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho)
.

Proof.

We show that we can build
\rho_{K}
by induction on
K
:
for
K=1
, there is nothing to do and
\rho_{1}=\rho
. Let
1<K<\ell
, and assume we have built
\rho_{K-1}=C_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{0},t^{0}}C_{1}\xrightarrow{v^{1},t^{1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n-1},t^{n-1}}C_{n}
such that
C_{n}=(\Gamma,L_{n})
and:

•

for all
v\in\{v_{K},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
,
L_{n}(v)=L(v)
;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq K-2
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})
;

•

for all
K\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho)
.

If
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
, there is nothing to do.

Assume
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
, then
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\geq 0
and
v_{K-1}
issues at least one transition in
\rho_{K-1}
after
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
. Let

j_{1}=\begin{cases}\min\{j>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})\mid C_{j}\xrightarrow{v_{K-1},t}C_{j+1}\text{ for some }t\in\Delta\}&\textrm{ if }b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})\\
\min{\{j\geq 0\mid C_{j}\xrightarrow{v_{i},t}C_{j+1}\text{ for some }t\in\Delta\textrm{ and for some }1\leq i\leq K-1\}}&\textrm{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We now build the following sequence of configurations
C^{\prime}_{j_{1}},\dots C^{\prime}_{n}
:

•

C^{\prime}_{j_{1}}=C^{\prime}_{j_{1}+1}=C_{j_{1}}
;

•

for
j_{1}+1\leq j<n
,
C^{\prime}_{j+1}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime}_{j+1})
and

–

L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L_{j+1}(v)
for all
v\in\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
,

–

L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-2}\}
,

–

L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K-1})
is defined as follows: if
v^{j}=v_{K}
and
t^{j}
is a broadcast transition of some message
m\in\Sigma
, and
(L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K-1}),?m,p)\in\Delta
for some
p \in Q
, then
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K-1})=p
, otherwise
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K-1})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K-1})
;

–

L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})
is defined as follows:

*

if
v^{j}=v_{K+1}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})\neq L_{j}(v_{K})
and
t^{j}
is a broadcast transition of some message
m\in\Sigma
, and
(L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K}),?m,p)\in\Delta
for some
p \in Q
, then
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=p
;

*

if
v^{j}=v_{K+1}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
, then
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
;

*

if
v^{j}=v_{K}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
, then
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
;

*

otherwise,
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})
.

At
j_{1}
, actions of process
v_{K-1}
(and hence actions of all processes in
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1}\}
will have no influence any more on
\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
, either
because
v_{K}
has emitted its first broadcast, and thus will only receive messages from
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1}\}
in its last reception phase (then
will never feed it to
v_{K+1}
), or because anyway the first broadcast of
v_{K-1}
happens to late (after the first broadcast of
v_{K}
). In order to prove the induction step, we have just built a sequence of configurations that ignore subsequent actions of
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1}\}
, starting from

j_{1}
, while
still being an execution. The state of the protocol in which
v_{K}
is during this part of the execution is either faithful to what it was in
\rho_{K-1}
, or

v_{K}
is in its last reception phase.

Formally, we now prove that, for all
j_{1}+1\leq j\leq n
,

1.

P_{1}(j)
: either

C^{\prime}_{j-1}\xrightarrow{v^{j-1},t^{j-1}}C^{\prime}_{j}
and
v^{j-1}\in\{v_{K},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
or
C^{\prime}_{j}=C^{\prime}_{j-1}
and
v^{j-1}\in\{v_{1},\dots v_{K}\}
,

2.

P_{2}(j)
:
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
or
L_{j}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
.

We prove
P_{1}(j)
and
P_{2}(j)
by induction on
j
.
For
j=j_{1}+1
,
P_{1}(j)
trivially holds as
C^{\prime}_{j_{1}+1}=C^{\prime}_{j_{1}}
and
C_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{v_{i},t}C_{j_{1}+1}
for
1\leq i\leq K-1
, by
construction. Let us prove now that
P_{2}(j_{1}+1)
holds: recall first that
L^{\prime}_{j_{1}+1}(v_{K})=L^{\prime}_{j_{1}}(v_{K})=L_{j_{1}}(v_{K})
.
Since
C_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{v_{i},t}C_{j_{1}+1}
, for some
1\leq i\leq K-1
, either
L_{j_{1}+1}(v_{K})=L_{j_{1}}(v_{K})
and
P_{2}(j)
holds, or
(L_{j_{1}}(v_{K}),?m,L_{j_{1}+1}(v_{K}))\in\Delta
and
t=(p,!!m,q)
. In that case
C_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{v_{K-1},t}C_{j_{1}+1}
. Since either
j_{1}>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
if

b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
, or
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
and
C_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{v_{K-1},t}C_{j_{1}+1}
with
t=(p,!!m,q)
,
then in any case
j_{1}>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
. Since
P
is 2-phase-bounded,
L_{j_{1}}(v_{K})\in Q_{1}^{b}\cup Q_{2}^{b}\cup Q_{2}^{r}
and so
L_{j_{1}+1}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
. Hence
P_{2}(j)
holds.

Let now
j_{1}+1\leq j<n
such that
P_{1}(j)
and
P_{2}(j)
hold.
We make the following observation.

Observation 48
.

Let
C_{1}\xrightarrow{v,t}C_{1}^{\prime}
with
C_{1}=(\Gamma,L_{1})
and
C^{\prime}_{1}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime}_{1})
. Consider
C_{2}=(\Gamma,L_{2})
,

C_{2}^{\prime}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime}_{2})
two configurations such that, for all
u\in\{v\}\cup\textsf{N}(v)
,
L_{2}(u)=L_{1}(u)
and
L_{2}^{\prime}(u)=L_{1}^{\prime}(u)
, and for all
u\nin\{v\}\cup\textsf{N}(v)
,
L^{\prime}_{2}(u)=L_{2}(u)
. Then
C_{2}\xrightarrow{v,t}C_{2}^{\prime}
.

The induction step is proved by a case analysis over
v^{j}\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
. (Recall that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j+1}
.)

•

Assume
v^{j}\in\{v_{K+2},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
. Then
\{v^{j}\}\cup\textsf{N}(v^{j})\subseteq\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
, and by construction of
C^{\prime}_{j}
and

C^{\prime}_{j+1}
, for all
v\in\{v^{j}\}\cup\textsf{N}(v^{j})
,
L^{\prime}_{j}(v)=L_{j}(v)
and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L_{j+1}(v)
. Then, nodes of
\{v^{j}\}\cup\textsf{N}(v^{j})

behave in the same way than in
\rho_{K-1}
. Let
v\nin\{v^{j}\}\cup\textsf{N}(v^{j})
and
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K}\}
. In that case, either

v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-2}\}
, and by construction,
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
, or
v\in\{v_{K-1},v_{K}\}
, and since
v^{j}\notin\{v_{K-2},v_{K-1},v_{K},v_{K+1}\}
, by construction,
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
. Let
v\nin\{v^{j}\}\cup\textsf{N}(v^{j})
such that
v\in\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
. In that case,
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L_{j+1}(v)=L_{j}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
. This allows us to conclude that
C^{\prime}_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}
thanks to
Observation

48
, and so
P_{1}(j+1)
holds. By
P_{2}(j)
, either
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
and we have just proved that
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})
, and that

L_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
. Then
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
and
P_{2}(j+1)
holds. Or
L_{j}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
and since
L_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
,

L_{j+1}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
. Hence,
P_{2}(j+1)
holds.

•

Assume that
v^{j}=v_{K+1}
, or that
v^{j}=v_{K}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
. By construction,
L_{j+1}(v^{j})=L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v^{j})
and
L_{j}(v^{j})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v^{j})
.
Observe that
v^{j}
has at most two neighbors
v,v^{\prime}
such that:

–

v=v_{K+2}
or
v=v_{K+1}
, and so by construction
L_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)
and
L_{j}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
;

–

v^{\prime}=v_{K}
or
v^{\prime}=v_{K-1}
.
If
v^{\prime}=v_{K-1}
or
v^{\prime}=v_{K}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})\neq L_{j}(v_{K})
, then by construction either
t^{j}
is a broadcast transition of some message
m\in\Sigma
and
(L^{\prime}_{j}(v^{\prime}),?m,L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v^{\prime}))\in\Delta
. Or
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v^{\prime})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v^{\prime})
.
If
v^{\prime}=v_{K}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
, then
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
by definition.

Furthermore, for all
v\nin\{v^{j}\}\cup\textsf{N}(v^{j})
, either

v\in\{v_{K+2},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L_{j+1}(v)=L_{j}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
, or
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-2}\}
and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
, or
v=v_{K-1}

(if
v^{j}=v_{K+1}
), and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
.
This allows us to conclude (with
Observation

48
) that
C^{\prime}_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}
and so
P_{1}(j+1)
holds. For
P_{2}(j+1)
, if
v^{j}=v_{K}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
, then by construction
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
and so
P_{2}(j+1)
trivially holds. If
v^{j}=v_{K+1}
, then either
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{K})
and so by construction,
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
, or
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})\neq L_{j}(v_{K})
and so by induction hypothesis
L_{j}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
, and since
P
is 2-phase-bounded,
L_{j+1}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
.

•

Assume that
v^{j}=v_{K}
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})\neq L_{j}(v_{K})
. Then, by
P_{2}(j)
,
L_{j}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
. Hence,
t
is an internal transition (it
cannot be a broadcast), and so
L_{j+1}(v)=L_{j}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)
for all

v\in\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
, and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)
for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots v_{K-2}\}
, and

L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K-1})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K-1})
and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})
. As a consequence,

C^{\prime}_{j+1}=C^{\prime}_{j}
and
P_{1}(j+1)
holds. Furthermore, by induction hypothesis,
L_{j}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
and so, as
P
is 2-phase-bounded, it holds that
L_{j+1}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
and we have proved
P_{2}(j+1)
.

•

Finally, assume that
v^{j}\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1}\}
. Then, for all
v\in\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
,
L_{j+1}(v)=L_{j}(v)
,
and
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L_{j+1}(v)
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v)=L_{j}(v)
by construction. For all
v^{j}\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1},v_{K}\}
,
L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v)=L^{\prime}_{j}(v)

and so
C^{\prime}_{j+1}=C^{\prime}_{j}
and
P_{1}(j)
holds. Furthermore, either
L_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})
or
(L_{j}(v_{K}),?m,L_{j+1}(v_{K}))\in\Delta
and

v^{j}=v_{K-1}
and
t^{j}=(L_{j}(v_{K-1}),!!m,L_{j+1}(v_{K-1}))
. In the first case,
by
P_{2}(j)
, either
L_{j}(v_{K})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})
and then
L_{j+1}(v_{K})=L_{j}(v_{k})=L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{K})=L^{\prime}_{j+1}(v_{K})
, or
L_{j}(v_{K})=L_{j+1}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
and so

P_{2}(j+1)
holds. In the second case,
j>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
. Indeed, either
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
and then
j>j_{1}>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
, or
j\geq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
. By definition of a 2-phase-bounded protocol,
L_{j}(v_{K})\in Q_{1}^{b}\cup Q_{2}^{b}\cup Q_{2}^{r}
hence,
L_{j+1}(v_{K})\in Q_{2}^{r}
and so
P_{2}(j+1)
holds.

Hence,
P_{1}(j)
and
P_{2}(j)
holds for all
j_{1}+1\leq j\leq n
.

We build a sequence of configurations
C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{n}
by letting
C^{\prime}_{i}=C_{i}
for all
0\leq i\leq j_{1}
.
Let us show now that
C^{\prime}_{0},\dots,C^{\prime}_{n}
that we have defined form the expected execution
\rho_{K}
.

•

Let
v\in\{v_{K+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
. By construction
L^{\prime}_{n}(v)=L_{n}(v)
. By induction hypothesis (on
K
),
L_{n}(v)=L(v)
.

•

Since
P_{1}(j)
holds for all
j_{1}+1\leq j\leq n
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K})<j_{1}
and, by definition of
j_{1}
, it implies that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K})
. Let
1\leq i<K-1
. By induction hypothesis,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})
.
If
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
, then
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-2},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})<j_{1}
. Thus,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-2},\rho_{K})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{K-2},\rho_{K-2})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K})
. Since, for all
1\leq i<K-2
,
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})<j_{1}
, and by
induction hypothesis,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})
, we deduce that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K})
.
If now
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K-1},\rho_{K-1})>b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{K},\rho_{K-1})
, then by
P_{1}(j)
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v,\rho_{K})=-1
, for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1}\}
. Obviously then, for all
1\leq i\leq K-1
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K})
.

•

Let
K+1\leq i\leq\ell
. Then, by
P_{1}(j)
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{K-1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{K-1})
if and only if, by induction hypothesis,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho)
.

∎

By symmetry, we get the following lemma.

Lemma 49
.

Let
\rho=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
with
C=(\Gamma,L)
and
1<K\leq\ell
.
There exists
\rho^{\prime}=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}
such that
C^{\prime}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime})
, and

•

for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{K-1}\}
,
L^{\prime}(v)=L(v)
;

•

for all
K+1\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime})
;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq K
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho)
.

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

46
.

Proof of
Lemma

46
.

From
\rho=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
with
C=(\Gamma,L)
we apply
Lemma

47
for
K=N-1
and we get an execution
\rho^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}
such that
C^{\prime}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime})
and

•

for all
v\in\{v_{N},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
,
L^{\prime}(v)=L(v)
;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq N-2
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime})
;

•

for all
N-1\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho)
.

Next, from
\rho^{\prime}
, we apply
Lemma

49
for
K=N+1
and we get an execution
\rho^{\prime\prime}=C^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime\prime}
such that
C^{\prime\prime}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime\prime})
and

•

for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N}\}
,
L^{\prime\prime}(v)=L^{\prime}(v)
;

•

for all
N+2\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime\prime})
;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq N+1
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime\prime})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime})
.

Hence,
\rho^{\prime\prime}
covers
q_{f}
as
L^{\prime\prime}(v_{N})=L^{\prime}(v_{N})=L(v_{N})=q_{f}
and

•

for all
N+2\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime\prime})
;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq N-2
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime\prime})
.

\square

Proof of
Lemma

20

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

20
.
We begin with three observations that hold regardless of whether
P
is 2-phase-bounded or not.
In a line
\Gamma=(V,E)
with
V=\{v_{1},\dots v_{\ell}\}
and
E=\{\langle v_{i},v_{i+1}\rangle\mid 1\leq i<\ell\}
, we define the distance between two nodes
v_{i},v_{j}
as
d(v_{i},v_{j})=|j-i|
.

Observation 50
.

Let
C,C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, and
u,v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
such that
d(v,u)\geq 3
. If
C\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{1}\xrightarrow{v,t^{\prime}}C^{\prime}
for two transitions
t,t^{\prime}\in\Delta
and a configuration
C_{1}\in\mathcal{C}
, then there exists
C_{2}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
C\xrightarrow{v,t^{\prime}}C_{2}\xrightarrow{u,t}C^{\prime}
.

Observation 51
.

Let
C,C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, and
u,v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
such that
d(u,v)\geq 2
. Then if
C\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{1}\xrightarrow{v,t^{\prime}}C^{\prime}
for two transitions
t,t^{\prime}\in\Delta
with one of the two transitions
internal
and a configuration
C_{1}\in\mathcal{C}
, then there exists
C_{2}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
C\xrightarrow{v,t^{\prime}}C_{2}\xrightarrow{u,t}C^{\prime}
.

Observation 52
.

Let
C,C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, and
u,v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
. Then if
C\xrightarrow{u,t}C_{1}\xrightarrow{v,t^{\prime}}C^{\prime}
for two internal transitions
t,t^{\prime}\in\Delta
and a configuration
C_{1}\in\mathcal{C}
, then there exists
C_{2}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
C\xrightarrow{v,t^{\prime}}C_{2}\xrightarrow{u,t}C^{\prime}
.

For the rest of this proof we let
\rho=C_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{0},t^{0}}C_{1}\xrightarrow{v^{1},t^{1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n-1},t^{n-1}}C_{n}
be the execution obtained from
Lemma

46
. We define
j_{1}:=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)+1
and
j_{2}=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)+1
.

Lemma 53
.

Let
0\leq j<j_{1}
be the maximal index such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}} C_{j+1}
, for some
v^{j}\in\{v_{N-1},v_{N},\dots,v_{\ell}\}

then,

C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j+1},t^{j+1}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{j_{1}-2}\xrightarrow{v_{N-3},t^{j_{1}}}C^{\prime}_{j_{1}-1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j_{1}}
.

Proof.

Let
0\leq j<j_{1}
be the maximal index such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j+1}
for some
v\in\{v_{N-1},v_{N},\dots v_{\ell}\}
.

Recall that
j_{1}=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)+1
, and recall that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-2},\rho)
, hence either
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-2},\rho)=-1
and so it never broadcast anything, either
j_{1}\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-2},\rho)
.
In both cases,
v_{N-2}
does not broadcast anything between
C_{0}
and
C_{j_{1}}
.

As
j
is maximal, for all
j<k<j_{1}
,
v^{k}\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N-2}\}
and if
v^{k}=v_{N-2}
,
t^{k}
is internal.
We prove by induction that for all
j\leq k<j_{1}
:

P(k)
: there exists
C^{\prime}_{j+1},\dots,C^{\prime}_{k}
such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j+1},t^{j+1}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}\rightarrow\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{k},t^{k}}C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+1}
.

For
k=j
, the induction property trivially holds.

Let
j\leq k<j_{1}-1
such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j+1},t^{j+1}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{k},t^{k}}C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+1}
. Let
C_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C_{k+2}
.

First case:
v^{j}=v_{N-1}
.
Then, since
j\leq k+1\leq j_{1}-1=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-2},\rho)\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-2},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-1},\rho)
, by
Lemma

46
,
t^{j}
cannot be a broadcast and is thus an internal transition.
If
v^{k+1}\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N-3}\}
, then
d(v_{N-1},v^{k+1})\geq 2
and we apply
Observation

51
, to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
. If
v^{k+1}=v_{N-2}
, then
t^{k+1}
is internal and we can hence apply
Observation

52
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
.

Second case:
v^{j}=v_{N}
.
Then, if
v^{k+1}\in\{v_{1},\dots v_{N-3}\}
and so
d(v_{N},v^{k+1})\geq 3
: We can apply
Observation

50
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
. Otherwise,
v^{k+1}=v_{N-2}
and
t^{k+1}
is internal and
d(v_{N},v_{N-2})=2
and so we can apply
Observation

51
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
.

Third case:
v^{j}\in\{v_{N+1},\dots v_{\ell}\}
.
Then
d(v^{j},v^{k+1})\geq 3
and so we can apply
Observation

50
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
.

∎

Lemma 54
.

Let
j_{1}\leq j<j_{2}
the
maximal
index such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}} C_{j+1}
, for some
v^{j}\in\{v_{N-2},v_{N-1},v_{N},v_{N+1}\}
, then,

C_{j_{1}}\rightarrow^{\ast}C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j+1},t^{j+1}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j+2},t^{j+2}}\cdots\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{j_{2}-2} \xrightarrow{v_{N+3},t^{j_{2}}}C^{\prime}_{j_{2}-1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j_{2}}
.

Proof.

Let
j_{1}\leq j<j_{2}
be the maximal index such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j+1}
for some
v\in\{v_{N-2},v_{N-1},v_{N},v_{N+1}\}
.

Recall that
j_{1}=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)+1
, and that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)\geq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-3},\rho)\geq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-4},\rho)
, and so by a simple inductive reasoning:
j_{1}>t_{\textsf{last}}(v,\rho)
for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N-3}\}
. Hence, for all
j_{1}\leq k<j_{2}
,
v^{k}\nin\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N-3}\}
.

Hence, as
j
is maximal, for all
j<k<j_{1}
,
v^{k}\in\{v_{N+2},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
.
We prove by induction that for all
j\leq k<j_{2}
, there exist
C^{\prime}_{j+1},\dots C^{\prime}_{k}
such that
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j+1},t^{j+1}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}\rightarrow\cdots C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+1}
.
For
k=j
, the induction property trivially holds. Let
j\leq k<j_{2}-1
such that there exist
C^{\prime}_{j+1},\dots C^{\prime}_{k}
with
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j+1},t^{j+1}}C^{\prime}_{j+1}\rightarrow\cdots C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+1}
. Denote
C_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C_{k+2}
.

First case:

v^{j}=v_{N+1}
. Then,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+2},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N+1},\rho)
by
Lemma

46
, and
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N+2},\rho)\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+2},\rho)
. Since
k+1\leq j_{2}-1=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)
,
k+1\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N+1},\rho)
, and so,
t^{j}
cannot be a broadcast, and is an internal transition.
If
v^{k+1}\in\{v_{N+3},\dots v_{\ell}\}
then
d(v_{N+1},v^{k+1})\geq 2
and we can apply
Observation

51
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
. Otherwise,
v^{k+1}=v_{N+2}
. Recall that
j_{2}=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)+1
and that
k+1\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N+2},\rho)
, hence
t^{k+1}
is an internal transition. Hence we can apply
Observation

52
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
.

Second case:

v^{j}=v_{N}
. Then, if
v^{k+1}\in\{v_{N+3},\dots v_{\ell}\}
and so
d(v_{N},v^{k+1})\geq 3
, we can apply

Observation

50
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
. Otherwise,
v^{k+1}=v_{N+2}
and
t^{k+1}
is internal and
d(v_{N},v_{N+2})=2
and so we can apply

Observation

51
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
.

Third case:
v^{j}\in\{v_{N-2},v_{N-1}\}
.
Then
d(v^{j},v^{k+1})\geq 3
and so we can apply
Observation

50
to get that there exists
C^{\prime}_{k+1}
such that
C^{\prime}_{k}\xrightarrow{v^{k+1},t^{k+1}}C^{\prime}_{k+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{k+2}
.

∎

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

20
.

Proof of
Lemma

20
.

Let

j_{1}(\rho):=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho)+1
and
j_{2}(\rho)=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho)+1
. We let
i_{1},\dots i_{k}
the indices
0\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}<j_{1}(\rho)
such that
C_{i_{j}}\xrightarrow{v,t_{j}}C_{i_{j}+1}
for some
v\in\{v_{N-1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
and
t_{j}\in\Delta
We denote
\textsf{EarlyActions}[\rho]
the number of such indices.

We build inductively
\rho_{0},\rho_{1},\dots\rho_{k}
such that for all
j
,
\rho_{j}=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{j}=(\Gamma,L^{j})
with:

•

P_{1}(j)
:
L^{j}(v_{N})=q_{f}
, and

•

P_{2}(j)
:
\textsf{EarlyActions}[\rho_{j}]=k-j
, and

•

P_{3}(j)
: the order of
Lemma

46
is preserved, i.e. for all
1\leq i\leq N-2
,

t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho_{j})
and for all
N+2\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho_{j})
.

For
j=0
, define
\rho_{0}=\rho
. It trivially satisfies the induction properties.

Let
0\leq j<k
and assume we have built
\rho_{j}
satisfying
P_{1}(j)
,
P_{2}(j)
and
P_{3}(j)
and denote it
\rho_{j}=C^{j}_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{0},t^{0}}C^{j}_{1}\xrightarrow{v^{1},t^{1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n-1}t^{n-1}}C^{j}_{n}=(\Gamma,L^{j}_{n})
.
Let
i
be the maximal index such that
i<j_{1}(\rho_{j})
and
C_{i}\xrightarrow{v^{i},t^{i}}C_{i+1}
for some
v^{i}\in\{v_{N-1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}

and
t\in\Delta
. From
Lemma

53
, there exists
C^{\prime}_{i+1},\dots C^{\prime}_{j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1}
such that
C^{j}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{j}_{i}\xrightarrow{v^{i+1},t^{i+1}}C^{\prime}_{i+1}\xrightarrow{v^{i+2},t^{i+2}}\cdots\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1}\xrightarrow{v^{i},t^{i}}C^{j}_{j_{1}(\rho_{j})}
, hence we define

\rho_{j+1}=C^{j}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{j}_{i}\xrightarrow{v^{i+1},t^{i+1}}C^{\prime}_{i+1}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1}\xrightarrow{v^{i},t^{i}}C^{j}_{j_{1}(\rho_{j})}\rightarrow^{\ast}C_{n}
.
By construction
P_{1}(j+1)
holds.
Furthermore,
j_{1}(\rho_{j+1})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho_{j+1})+1=j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1
and so
\textsf{EarlyActions}[\rho_{j+1}]=\textsf{EarlyActions}[\rho_{j}]-1=k-j-1
and so
P_{2}(j+1)
holds.

We denote
v^{i}=v_{r}
.
Recall that
v_{r}\in\{v_{N-1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
.
Observe that for all
1\leq k\leq\ell
, it holds that:

•

if
k<\ell
and
k\nin\{r-1,r\}
, then
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j+1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k+1},\rho_{j+1})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k+1},\rho_{j})
;

•

if
k>0
and
k\nin\{r,r+1\}
, then
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j+1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho_{j+1})
if and only if
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho_{j})
.

Hence, by
P_{3}(j)
, for all
1\leq k\leq N-2
, if
k\nin\{r-1,r\}

t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j+1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k+1},\rho_{j+1})
and for all
N+2\leq k\leq\ell
, if
k\nin\{r,r+1\}
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho_{j})
.

Let now
1\leq k\leq N-2
, such that
k\in\{r-1,r\}
. As
N-1\leq r\leq\ell
,
k=N-2
and
r=N-1
.
As
j_{1}(\rho_{j})>i\geq 0
, from
P_{3}(j)
, we get that
j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-3},\rho_{j})\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho_{j})<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j})\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j})<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})
. Hence
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j+1})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j})
, and
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})
.

Let now
N+2\leq k\leq\ell
, such that
k\in\{r,r+1\}
, hence,
N+1\leq r\leq\ell
.
If
k=r
, then notice that either
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})>i
or
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})=i
. In the first case,
as for all
i<i_{2}<j_{1}(\rho_{j})
,
v^{i_{2}}\nin\{v_{N+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
, it holds that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})>j_{1}(\rho_{j+1})
, and so
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})
, and,
from
P_{3}(j)
,
j_{1}(\rho_{j})\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j})=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j+1})
.
If
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})=i
, then
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})=j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1
. As for all
i<i_{2}<j_{1}(\rho_{j})
,
v^{i_{2}}\nin\{v_{N+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
, it holds that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})\leq j_{1}(\rho_{j})-1\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j})=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j+1})
. Hence,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r-1},\rho_{j+1})
.

If
k=r+1
, then, as for all
i<i_{2}<j_{1}(\rho_{j})
,
v^{i_{2}}\nin\{v_{N+1},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
, it holds that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j+1})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j})
and if
t^{i}
is internal,
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})
.
Otherwise,
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{r},\rho_{j+1})
. From
P_{3}(j)
, it holds that
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j+1})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{k},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho_{j})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho_{j+1})
.
Hence
P_{3}(j+1)
holds.

Hence, we build
\rho_{k}
such that
\rho_{k}=C^{k}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{k}_{j_{1}(\rho_{k})}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{k}_{n}
and
P_{1}(k)
,
P_{2}(k)
and
P_{3}(k)
hold.

With the same reasoning between
C_{j_{1}}
and
C_{j_{2}}
, and applying this time
Lemma

54
, we finally get an execution
\rho^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{j_{1}(\rho^{\prime})}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{j_{2}(\rho^{\prime})}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{n}
such that:

•

for all
1\leq i\leq N-2
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i+1},\rho^{\prime})
;

•

for all
N+2\leq i\leq\ell
,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{i},\rho^{\prime})\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{i-1},\rho^{\prime})
.

•

nodes issuing transitions between
C^{\prime}_{0}
and
C^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}_{1}}
belong to
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N-3},v_{N-2}\}
and
v_{N-2}
only performs internal transitions (because
j_{1}(\rho^{\prime})=t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho^{\prime})+1\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-2},\rho^{\prime})
;

•

nodes issuing transitions between
C^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}_{1}}
and
C^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}_{2}}
belong to
\{v_{N+2},v_{N+3},\dots,v_{\ell}\}
and
v_{N+2}
only performs internal transitions;

It is left to prove that in
C^{\prime}_{j_{2}(\rho^{\prime})}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{n}
, only nodes in
\{v_{N-2},v_{N-1},v_{N},v_{N+1},v_{N+2}\}
issue transitions.
Assume a node
v\nin\{v_{N-2},v_{N-1},v_{N},v_{N+1},v_{N+2}\}
issues a transition between
C^{\prime}_{j_{2}(\rho^{\prime})}
and
C^{\prime}_{n}
, hence by definition,
t_{\textsf{last}}(v,\rho^{\prime})\geq j_{2}(\rho^{\prime})
.
Either
v\in\{v_{1},\dots v_{N-3}\}
and so
t_{\textsf{last}}(v,\rho^{\prime})\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N-3},\rho^{\prime})<j_{1}(\rho^{\prime})<j_{2}(\rho^{\prime})
and we reach a contradiction. Or
v\in\{v_{N+3},\dots v_{\ell}\}
and so
t_{\textsf{last}}(v,\rho^{\prime})\leq t_{\textsf{last}}(v_{N+3},\rho^{\prime})<j_{2}(\rho^{\prime})
and we reach a contradiction.

\square

E.2.2
Completeness of the algorithm

This part is dedicated to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 55
.

If
q_{f}
is coverable with a line topology, then there exist
q_{1},q_{2}\in H
such that
C_{q_{1},q_{2}}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
and
C=(\Gamma_{5},L)
with
L(v_{3})=q_{f}
.

If
q_{f}
is coverable, then let
\rho=C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C_{j_{1}}\rightarrow^{\ast}C_{j_{2}}\rightarrow^{\ast}C_{n}
be the execution obtained from
Lemma

20
with
j_{1}=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-3},\rho)+1
and
j_{2}=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N+3},\rho)+1
. We denote
C_{i}=(\Gamma,L_{i})
for all
0\leq i\leq n
.

We start by proving that
L_{j_{1}}(v_{N-2})\in H
and
L_{j_{2}}(v_{N+2})\in H
.
We need three preliminaries lemmas.

Lemma 56
.

For all
0\leq j\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{1},\rho)
,
(L_{j}(v_{1}),q_{in})\in S
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma by induction on
j
.
For
j=0
,
L_{0}(v_{1})=q_{in}
and by construction
(q_{in},q_{in})\in S
.
Let
0\leq j<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{1},\rho)
such that
(L_{j}(v_{1}),q_{in})\in S
.
Denote
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j+1}
. Observe that either
L_{j}(v_{1})=L_{j+1}(v_{1})
(and there is nothing to do), or
v^{j}\in\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
.
As
j<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{1},\rho)
, from
Item

1
of
Lemma

20
, it holds that
j<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{1},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{2},\rho)
. Hence
t^{j}
is internal and if
v^{j}=v_{2}
,
L_{j}(v_{1})=L_{j+1}(v_{1})
. Otherwise,
v^{j}=v_{1}
and
(L_{j}(v_{1}),q_{in})\in S
. Hence,
(L_{j+1}(v_{1}),q_{in})\in S
by definition of
S
.

∎

We adopt the convention that
L_{-1}(v)=q_{in}
for all
v
.
We prove the following lemma by induction on
k
.

Lemma 57
.

For all
1<k\leq N-2
,
if
L_{b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho)}(v_{k-1})\in H
, then
L_{j}(v_{k})\in H
for all
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho) \leq j\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k},\rho)
.

Proof.

Let
1<k\leq N-2
such that
L_{b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho)}(v_{k-1})\in H
, we prove the lemma by induction.
For ease of readability, denote
j^{k-1}=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k-1},\rho)
and
j^{k}=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k},\rho)
.
By definition of
H
, there exists
q\in Q
such that
(L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}),q)\in S
.

First let us show that for all
0\leq j\leq j^{k-1}
,
(L_{j}(v_{k}),L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}))\in S
: for
j=0
, as
(L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}),q)\in S
, by definition of
S
,
(q_{in},L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}))\in S
.
Let now
0\leq j<j^{k-1}
and denote
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j+1}
.
Recall that in
\rho
, as
j<j^{k-1}\leq j_{1}
, it holds that
v^{j}\in\{v_{1},\dots v_{N-2}\}
(
Item

1
of
Lemma

20
). Furthermore, as
j<j^{k-1}
, then
j<b_{\textsf{first}}(v,\rho)
for all
v\in\{v_{k-1},\dots,v_{N-2}\}
(
Item

2
of
Lemma

20
). Hence, either
t^{j}
is an internal transition, or
v^{j}\in\{v_{1},\dots v_{k-2}\}
. In the latter case,
L_{j+1}(v_{k})=L_{j}(v_{k})
.
Otherwise, either
v^{j}\neq v_{k}
and so
L_{j+1}(v_{k})=L_{j}(v_{k})
and there is nothing to do, or
v^{j}=v_{k}
and so
(L_{j}(v_{k}),\tau,L_{j+1}(v_{k}))\in\Delta
and so, as
(L_{j}(v_{k}),L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}))\in S
,
(L_{j+1}(v_{k}),L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}))\in S
by definition of
S
.

Hence
(L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k}),L_{j^{k-1}}(v_{k-1}))\in S
. We now prove that for all
j^{k-1}\leq j\leq j^{k}
,
(L_{j}(v_{k}),L_{j}(v_{k+1}))\in S
. As we just proved it for
j=j^{k-1}
, let
j^{k-1}\leq j<j^{k}
, and denote
C_{j}\xrightarrow{v^{j},t^{j}}C_{j+1}
. Note that from
Lemma

20
, we get that
(a)
v^{j}\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{N-2}\}
as
j<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k},\rho)\leq j_{1}
; (b) as
j\geq j^{k-1}
, it holds that
j\geq t_{\textsf{last}}(v,\rho)
for all
v\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k-2}\}
, hence
v^{j}\nin\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k-2}\}
; and (c) if
v^{j}\in\{v_{k},\dots v_{N-2}\}
, then
t^{j}
is an internal transition as
j<b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{k},\rho)\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v,\rho)
for all
v\in\{v_{k},\dots v_{N-2}\}
.

Overall, we get that either
v^{j}=v_{k-1}
, or
v^{j}\in\{v_{k},\dots,v_{N-2}\}
and
t^{j}
is internal.
In the first case, all cases (
t^{j}
is an internal transition,
t^{j}
is a broadcast transition and the message is received by
v_{k}
, or
t^{j}
is a broadcast transition and the message is not received by
v_{k}
) are covered by our definition of
S
.
In the latter case, either
v^{j}\neq v_{k}
and
v_{k}
and
v_{k-1}
remain on the same states, or
v^{j}=v_{k}
and this case is covered by our definition of
S
(
t^{j}
is internal).

∎

Hence, as
L_{b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{1},\rho)}(v_{1})\in H
from
Lemma

56
, by applying inductively
Lemma

57
, we get that
L_{j}(v_{N-2})\in H
for all
b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-3},\rho) \leq j\leq b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-2},\rho)
. As
j_{1}=b_{\textsf{first}}(v_{N-3},\rho)+1
, then
L_{j_{1}}(v_{N-2})\in H
.

With a similar reasoning on
v_{N+2}
and between
C_{j_{1}}
and
C_{j_{2}}
, we get the following lemma.

Lemma 58
.

L_{j_{1}}(v_{N-2})\in H
and
L_{j_{2}}(v_{N+2})\in H
.

Denote
q_{1}=L_{j_{1}}(v_{N-2})
and
q_{2}=L_{j_{2}}(v_{N+2})
. Denote
C_{j_{2}}\xrightarrow{v^{j_{2}},t^{j_{2}}}C_{j_{2}+1}\xrightarrow{v^{j_{2}+1},t^{j_{2}+1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n-1},t^{n-1}}C_{n}
. Recall that from
Item

1
of
Lemma

20
,
v^{j_{2}},\dots v^{n-1}\in\{v_{N-2},v_{N-1},v_{N},v_{N+1},v_{N+2}\}
. Denote
C_{q_{1},q_{2}}=(\Gamma_{5},L_{q_{1},q_{2}})
, and observe that
L_{q_{1},q_{2}}(v_{i})=L_{j_{2}}(v_{N-3+i})
for all
1\leq i\leq 5
.
Hence,
C_{q_{1},q_{2}}=C^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{v_{2}^{0},t_{2}^{0}}C^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{v_{2}^{1},t_{2}^{1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v_{2}^{n-j_{2}-1},t_{2}^{n-j_{2}-1}}C^{\prime}_{n-j_{2}}
where for all
0 \leq j\leq n-j_{2}
:
C^{\prime}_{j}=(\Gamma_{5},L^{\prime}_{j})
and
L^{\prime}_{j}(v_{i})=L_{j_{2}+j}(v_{N-3+i})
for all
1\leq i\leq 5
. And for all
0 \leq j<n-j_{2}
:
t_{2}^{j}=t^{j_{2}+j}
and if
v^{j_{2}+j}=v_{N-3+i}
for some
1\leq i\leq 5
, then
v_{2}^{j}=v_{i}
.

Hence,
C_{q_{1},q_{2}}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}_{n-j_{2}}
with
L^{\prime}_{n-j_{2}}(v_{3})=L_{n}(v_{N})=q_{f}
.
This concludes the proof of
Lemma

55
.

E.2.3
Correctness of the algorithm

This part is devoted to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 59
.

If there exist
q_{1},q_{2}\in H
,
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{5})
, such that
C_{q_{1},q_{2}}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
and
C=(\Gamma_{5},L)
with
L(v)=q_{f}
, then there exist
\Gamma\in\textsf{Lines}
,
C_{0}\in\mathcal{I}
,
C^{\prime}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}
such that
C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}
and
L^{\prime}(v)=q_{f}
.

We start by proving the following lemma.

Lemma 60
.

For all
q\in H
, there exists
k\in{\rm Nature}
such that
q
is coverable with a line topology
\Gamma=(\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\},\{\langle v_{i},v_{i+1}\rangle\mid 1\leq i<k\})
and vertex
v_{1}
.

Proof.

We will in fact prove that for all
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S
, there exists
\Gamma_{q_{1},q_{2}}\in\textsf{Lines}
such that
\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{1},q_{2}})=\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\}
,
\textsf{E}(\Gamma_{q_{1},q_{2}})=\{\langle v_{i},v_{i+1}\rangle\mid 1\leq i<k\}
for some
k\in{\rm Nature}
with
k\geq 2
and there exists
C_{0}\in\mathcal{I}
,
C=(\Gamma_{q_{1},q_{2}},L)
with
C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
and
L(v_{1})=q_{1}
and
L(v_{2})=q_{2}
.

Denote
S_{0},\dots,S_{K}
the subsets defined by the algorithm to build
S
. We have that
S=S_{N}
.
We prove the property to be true for all
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S_{i}
by induction on
0\leq i\leq N
.
For
i=0
,
S_{0}=\{(q_{in},q_{in})\}
, this is trivial: define
\Gamma_{q_{in},q_{in}}=(\{v_{1},v_{2}\},\{\langle v_{1},v_{2}\rangle\})
and take the initial configuration
C=(\Gamma_{q_{in},q_{in}},L_{0})
with
L_{0}(v_{1})=L_{0}(v_{2})=q_{in}
.
Let
0\leq i<K
, and assume we proved the property to be true for all
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S_{i}
.
Let
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S_{i+1}\in S_{i}
.

•

if there exists
(q_{1},p_{2})\in S_{i}
such that
(p_{2},\tau,q_{2})\in\Delta
, let
\Gamma_{q_{1},p_{2}}\in\textsf{Lines}
, and
C_{0}
and
C=(\Gamma_{q_{1},p_{2}},L)
obtained from the induction hypothesis such that
C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
and
L(v_{1})=q_{1}
and
L(v_{2})=p_{2}
. Then,
C\xrightarrow{v_{2},(p_{2},\tau,q_{2})}C^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime}=(\Gamma_{q_{1},p_{2}},L^{\prime})
with
L^{\prime}(v_{1})=q_{1}
and
L^{\prime}(v_{2})=q_{2}
;

•

if there exists
(p_{1},q_{2})\in S_{i}
such that
(p_{1},\tau,q_{1})\in\Delta
, the reasoning is the same as in the previous case;

•

if there exists
(p_{1},p_{2})\in S_{i}
and
m\in\Sigma
such that
(p_{1},?m,q_{1})\in\Delta
and
(p_{2},!!m,q_{2})\in\Delta
, we use a similar reasoning as in the previous cases;

•

if there exists
(q_{1},p_{2})\in S_{i}
and
m\in\Sigma
such that
(p_{2},!!m,q_{2})\in\Delta
and
m\nin R(q_{1})
, we use a similar reasoning as in the previous cases;

•

if there exists
q_{3}
such that
(q_{2},q_{3})\in S_{i}
and
q_{1}=q_{in}
, then let
\Gamma_{q_{2},q_{3}}
,
C_{0}
and
C=(\Gamma_{q_{2},q_{3}},L)
obtained from the induction hypothesis such that
C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
and
L(v_{1})=q_{2}
and
L(v_{2})=q_{3}
. Define
\Gamma_{q_{1},q_{2}}=(\{v^{\prime}_{1}\}\cup\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{2},q_{3}}),\{\langle v^{\prime}_{1},v_{1}\rangle\}\cup\textsf{E}(\Gamma_{q_{2},q_{3}}))
. Denote
C_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{0},t^{0}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n-1},t^{n-1}}C_{n}=C
. Recall that as
L_{n}(v_{1})\in H
,
L_{n}(v_{1})\in Q_{0}\cup Q_{1}^{r}
, hence for all
0\leq i\leq n-1
, if
v^{i}=v_{1}
then
t^{i}
is internal. Hence, there exists
C^{\prime}_{0}\dots C^{\prime}_{n}
such that
C^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{0},t^{0}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n-1},t^{n-1}}C^{\prime}_{n}
and: for all
0\leq i<n-1
, with
C^{\prime}_{i}=(\Gamma_{q_{1},q_{2}},L^{\prime}_{i})
and
C_{i}=(\Gamma_{q_{2},q_{3}})
, and for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{2},q_{3}})
,
L^{\prime}_{i}(v)=L_{i}(v)
and
L^{\prime}_{i}(v^{\prime}_{1})=q_{in}
. Hence
L^{\prime}_{n}(v^{\prime}_{1})=q_{in}
and
L^{\prime}_{n}(v_{1})=q_{2}
.

∎

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

59
.

Proof of
Lemma

59
.

Let
q_{1},q_{2}\in H
, from
Lemma

60
, let
\Gamma_{q_{1}}=(\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\},\{\langle v_{i},v_{i+1}\rangle\mid 1\leq i<k\})
, and
\Gamma_{q_{2}}=(\{v^{\prime}_{1},\dots,v^{\prime}_{n}\},\{\langle v^{\prime}_{i},v^{\prime}_{i+1}\rangle\mid 1\leq i<n\})
the two line topologies such that
q_{1}
is coverable with
v_{1}
and
q_{2}
with
v^{\prime}_{1}
.
Denote
C_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C
with
C=(\Gamma_{q_{1}},L)
and
L(v_{1})=q_{1}
and
C^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow^{\ast}C^{\prime}
with
C^{\prime}=(\Gamma_{q_{2}},L^{\prime})
and
L^{\prime}(v^{\prime}_{1})=q_{2}
. As
q_{1},q_{2}\in H
and
H\subseteq Q_{0}\cup Q_{1}^{r}
,
v_{1}
does not broadcast anything between
C_{0}
and
C
and
v^{\prime}_{1}
does not broadcast anything between
C^{\prime}_{0}
and
C^{\prime}
.

Define
\Gamma=(V,E)
such that
V=\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{1}})\cup\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{2}})\cup\{u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\}
and
E=\textsf{E}(\Gamma_{q_{1}})\cup\textsf{E}(\Gamma_{q_{2}})\cup\{\langle v_{1},u_{2}\rangle,\langle u_{2},u_{3}\rangle,\langle u_{3},u_{4}\rangle,\langle u_{4},v^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\}
.
Note that
v_{1}
and
v^{\prime}_{1}
are not neighbors, and that each node in
\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{1}})
[resp. in
\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{2}})
] has the same neighborhood as before, except for
v_{1}
, and
v^{\prime}_{1}
which have an additional new neighbor, respectively
u_{2}
and
u_{4}
.
Denote
C^{\prime\prime}_{0}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime\prime}_{0})
with
L^{\prime\prime}_{0}(v)=q_{in}
for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma)
.

Denote
C_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{1},t^{1}}C_{1}\xrightarrow{v^{2},t^{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n_{1}},t^{n_{1}}}C_{n_{1}}=C
and
C^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{\prime 1},t^{\prime 1}}C^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{v^{\prime 2},t^{\prime 2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{\prime n_{2}},t^{\prime n_{2}}}C^{\prime}_{n_{2}}=C^{\prime}
.
Then
C^{\prime\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{v^{1},t^{1}}C^{\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{v^{2},t^{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{n_{1}},t^{n_{1}}}C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}}\xrightarrow{v^{\prime 1},t^{\prime 1}}C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+1}\xrightarrow{v^{\prime 2},t^{\prime 2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{v^{\prime n_{2}},t^{\prime n_{2}}}C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}
with
C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}})
and
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}(v)=L_{n_{1}}(v)
for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{1}})
and
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}(v)=L^{\prime}_{n_{2}}(v)
for all
v\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{q_{2}})
and
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}(v)=q_{in}
for all
v\in\{u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\}
.
Hence
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}(v_{1})=q_{1}
and
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}(v^{\prime}_{1})=q_{2}
.

Finally, rename
\Gamma_{5}=(\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4},u_{5}\},\{\langle u_{i},u_{i+1}\rangle\mid 1\leq i<5\})
, and
v_{1}
by
u_{1}
and
v^{\prime}_{1}
by
u_{5}
.
Denote
C^{5}_{q_{1},q_{2}}\xrightarrow{u^{1},\delta^{1}}C^{5}_{1}\xrightarrow{u^{2},\delta^{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{u^{n_{3}},\delta^{n_{3}}}C^{5}_{n_{3}}
. Denote
C^{5}_{q_{1},q_{2}}=(\Gamma_{5},L_{q_{1},q_{2}})
and
C^{5}_{n_{3}}=(\Gamma_{5},L^{5}_{n_{3}})

Note that for all
u\in\textsf{V}(\Gamma_{5})
,
L_{q_{1},q_{2}}(u)=L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}(u)
. Hence,
C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}}\xrightarrow{u^{1},\delta^{1}}C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}+1}\xrightarrow{u^{2},\delta^{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{u^{n_{3}},\delta^{n_{3}}}C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}
with
C^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}=(\Gamma,L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}})
and
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}(v)=L_{n_{1}}(v)
for all
v\in\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4},u_{5}\}
.
Hence
L^{\prime\prime}_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}(u_{3})=q_{f}
.

\square
